The company has approximately 22 employees and an estimated annual revenue of ~$1.4M (per ZoomInfo/LeadIQ data). UMC develops and sells Team Chart Concept (TCC), an integrated EHR and practice management system targeted at ambulatory medical practices. They serve solo practitioners, mid-sized clinics, and hospital-based groups across disciplines including primary care, internal medicine, dermatology, OB/GYN, general surgery, urgent care, and therapy practices. The intended users are medical office personnel including RNs, providers, admins, and support staff.\n     9→\n    10→In 2006, UMC created a subsidiary brand called **CorrecTek**, which provides correctional healthcare EHR and eMAR (electronic Medication Administration Record) software for jails, prisons, and juvenile detention centers. CorrecTek has its own product called \"Spark\" which is separately ONC-certified. CorrecTek appears to be a distinct product line from TCC, though they share the same parent company.\n    11→\n    12→UMC also provides medical billing services — \"beginning to end medical billing services\" including claims, coding, billing, and financial reporting — suggesting the company functions as both a software vendor and a billing services provider.\n    13→\n    14→UMC is a very small vendor with no presence on major software review platforms (G2, Capterra, Software Advice). There are no publicly available third-party reviews of TCC. The company appears to have a regional customer base, likely concentrated in the southeastern United States given its Kentucky headquarters and its documented integration with the Kentucky Health Information Exchange (KHIE) and the Kentucky Cancer Registry.\n    15→\n    16→## Product: Team Chart Concept (TCC)\n    17→\n    18→CHPL IDs: 10227\n    19→\n    20→### What It Is\n    21→\n    22→Team Chart Concept (TCC) version 7.1 is an integrated EHR and practice management system, certified on December 26, 2019. The vendor describes it as \"a full-featured suite, including electronic health records, practice management, high-level clinical data and interoperability.\" It is designed as a single platform that combines clinical charting and administrative/billing functions. The system is available as either on-premises or cloud-hosted deployment.\n    23→\n    24→TCC is certified for a broad set of 26+ ONC criteria spanning clinical data ((a)(1)-(a)(14)), transitions of care ((b)(1)), EHI export ((b)(10)), FHIR API ((g)(7)-(g)(10)), and public health reporting ((h)(1)). This indicates the certified module is a comprehensive clinical EHR, not a narrow specialty or single-function module.\n    25→\n    26→The vendor positions TCC as \"extremely powerful, flexible, and sophisticated\" — not a \"cookie-cutter EHR\" — and notes that significant training (10-15 hours per person, 20 hours for financial staff) and configuration is required to get full value. Implementation takes 2-3 months for small practices.\n    27→\n    28→### Users & Market\n    29→\n    30→TCC serves nurse practitioners and physicians across multiple disciplines: primary care, internal medicine, dermatology, OB/GYN, general surgery, urgent care, and therapy practices. The vendor's FAQ also mentions doctors, medical providers, therapists, nurse practitioners, and physician assistants as target users. The system is designed to \"engage everyone in your office, including the clerical staff, clinical practitioners, nurses and even patients.\"\n    31→\n    32→The vendor serves solo practitioners, mid-sized clinics, and hospital-based groups. No specific customer counts are publicly available. Given the company's size (~22 employees, ~$1.4M revenue), the customer base is likely small — probably dozens to low hundreds of practices rather than thousands.\n    33→\n    34→The vendor has a documented integration with the Kentucky Health Information Exchange (KHIE) and was described as \"the nation's first EHR to electronically submit cancer data to a state registry\" (Kentucky Cancer Registry), suggesting early adoption and strong ties to Kentucky healthcare infrastructure.\n    35→\n    36→### Modules & Functionality\n    37→\n    38→Based on vendor website pages (TCC Features, configurable workflow\n    42→- Rapid documentation of encounter notes with customizable clinical templates\n    43→- Health maintenance alerts and reminders\n    44→- Risk factor analysis\n    45→- Windows login, TCC login, department-based access controls\n    94→- HIPAA compliant with encryption\n    95→\n    96→### Data & Content\n    97→\n    98→Based on the features and modules documented vital signs, health maintenance records, implantable device lists, clinical decision support rules and alerts, clinical quality measure data.\n   101→\n   102→**Order data:** Lab orders and results The company has approximately 22 employees and an estimated annual revenue of ~$1.4M (per ZoomInfo/LeadIQ data). UMC develops and sells Team Chart Concept (TCC), an integrated EHR and practice management system targeted at ambulatory medical practices. They serve solo practitioners, mid-sized clinics, and hospital-based groups across disciplines including primary care, internal medicine, dermatology, OB/GYN, general surgery, urgent care, and therapy practices. The intended users are medical office personnel including RNs, providers, admins, and support staff.\n\nIn 2006, UMC created a subsidiary brand called **CorrecTek**, which provides correctional healthcare EHR and eMAR (electronic Medication Administration Record) software for jails, prisons, and juvenile detention centers. CorrecTek has its own product called \"Spark\" which is separately ONC-certified. CorrecTek appears to be a distinct product line from TCC, though they share the same parent company.\n\nUMC also provides medical billing services — \"beginning to end medical billing services\" including claims, coding, billing, and financial reporting — suggesting the company functions as both a software vendor and a billing services provider.\n\nUMC is a very small vendor with no presence on major software review platforms (G2, Capterra, Software Advice). There are no publicly available third-party reviews of TCC. The company appears to have a regional customer base, likely concentrated in the southeastern United States given its Kentucky headquarters and its documented integration with the Kentucky Health Information Exchange (KHIE) and the Kentucky Cancer Registry.\n\n## Product: Team Chart Concept (TCC)\n\nCHPL IDs: 10227\n\n### What It Is\n\nTeam Chart Concept (TCC) version 7.1 is an integrated EHR and practice management system, certified on December 26, 2019. The vendor describes it as \"a full-featured suite, including electronic health records, practice management, high-level clinical data and interoperability.\" It is designed as a single platform that combines clinical charting and administrative/billing functions. The system is available as either on-premises or cloud-hosted deployment.\n\nTCC is certified for a broad set of 26+ ONC criteria spanning clinical data ((a)(1)-(a)(14)), transitions of care ((b)(1)), EHI export ((b)(10)), FHIR API ((g)(7)-(g)(10)), and public health reporting ((h)(1)). This indicates the certified module is a comprehensive clinical EHR, not a narrow specialty or single-function module.\n\nThe vendor positions TCC as \"extremely powerful, flexible, and sophisticated\" — not a \"cookie-cutter EHR\" — and notes that significant training (10-15 hours per person, 20 hours for financial staff) and configuration is required to get full value. Implementation takes 2-3 months for small practices.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nTCC serves nurse practitioners and physicians across multiple disciplines: primary care, internal medicine, dermatology, OB/GYN, general surgery, urgent care, and therapy practices. The vendor's FAQ also mentions doctors, medical providers, therapists, nurse practitioners, and physician assistants as target users. The system is designed to \"engage everyone in your office, including the clerical staff, clinical practitioners, nurses and even patients.\"\n\nThe vendor serves solo practitioners, mid-sized clinics, and hospital-based groups. No specific customer counts are publicly available. Given the company's size (~22 employees, ~$1.4M revenue), the customer base is likely small — probably dozens to low hundreds of practices rather than thousands.\n\nThe vendor has a documented integration with the Kentucky Health Information Exchange (KHIE) and was described as \"the nation's first EHR to electronically submit cancer data to a state registry\" (Kentucky Cancer Registry), suggesting early adoption and strong ties to Kentucky healthcare infrastructure.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor website pages (TCC Features, Capabilities, and mandatory disclosures), TCC includes the following modules and capabilities:\n\n**Clinical / EHR:**\n- Electronic health records with button-based navigation and configurable workflow\n- Rapid documentation of encounter notes with customizable clinical templates\n- Health maintenance alerts and reminders\n- Risk factor analysis\n- Implantable device list management\n- Decision support interventions (clinical decision support)\n- Drug-drug and drug-allergy interaction checking\n- Clinical quality measures dashboard with automated tracking (6 CQMs supported)\n- Support for dictation, voice recognition, and handwriting recognition input methods\n- Multi-user simultaneous chart access\n\n**Prescribing:**\n- Electronic prescribing (e-prescribing) — via **NewCrop** (a third-party e-prescribing service by DrFirst), listed as an add-on with per-prescriber monthly fees\n\n**Orders:**\n- Computerized provider order entry (CPOE) for medications, laboratory, and diagnostic imaging\n- Bi-directional lab connectivity for orders and results\n- Interfacing with labs, pharmacies, rehabilitation and post-acute care facilities\n\n**Practice Management & Billing:**\n- Appointment scheduling for multiple providers, locations, and equipment\n- Integrated billing with \"full HIPAA compliant billing routines\"\n- **SmartCoder** claims scrubbing tool\n- ICD-10 ready\n- PQRS-capable\n- Standard and ad hoc reporting, plus \"User Defined Records\" for custom reportability\n\n**Document Management:**\n- Integrated document management system for patient charts, office forms, and documents\n- Historical data scanning and digitization services (offered as a service)\n\n**Patient Engagement:**\n- Patient portal — via **Medfusion** (third-party), listed as an add-on with monthly service fees\n- Partnership with **Bridge** (BridgeInteract) for enhanced patient engagement, mobile accessibility, and patient communication\n- Direct patient communication features\n\n**Interoperability & Data Exchange:**\n- Transitions of Care (C-CDA)\n- Direct Messaging — listed as an add-on with per-address monthly fees\n- Health Information Exchange connectivity (documented KHIE integration)\n- HL7 messaging and Clinical Document Architecture (CDA) support\n- FHIR API access (Application Access API) — listed as an add-on with monthly service fee\n- Multi-facility connectivity with labs, pharmacies, hospitals, imaging centers\n\n**Public Health Reporting:**\n- Cancer registry reporting (historically first to Kentucky Cancer Registry)\n- Certified for (h)(1) — transmission to public health agencies\n\n**Platform:**\n- Windows-based with Windows Tablet support for mobile access\n- Ribbon-style interface\n- On-premises or cloud-hosted deployment\n- Multi-level security: Windows login, TCC login, department-based access controls\n- HIPAA compliant with encryption\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the features and modules documented above, TCC manages the following categories of data:\n\n**Clinical data:** Patient demographics, encounter notes, problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists, immunization records, vital signs, health maintenance records, implantable device lists, clinical decision support rules and alerts, clinical quality measure data.\n\n**Order data:** Lab orders and results (bi-directional), medication orders, diagnostic imaging orders. Lab connectivity implies storage of lab results.\n\n**Prescribing data:** E-prescriptions via NewCrop integration, medication history, drug interaction checking data.\n\n**Practice management data:** Appointment schedules (multiple providers/locations/equipment), billing records, claims data (with SmartCoder scrubbing), financial reports, ICD-10 codes, PQRS quality data.\n\n**Document management:** Scanned documents, office forms, patient chart documents, digitized historical records.\n\n**Patient engagement data:** Patient portal interactions (via Medfusion), patient communications (via Bridge integration), direct messaging.\n\n**Interoperability data:** C-CDA documents, HL7 messages, CDA documents, FHIR resources, health information exchange transactions, public health submissions.\n\n**Notable observations on data scope:**\n- E-prescribing (NewCrop), patient portal (Medfusion), direct messaging, and FHIR API access are all listed as separate add-ons with additional fees on the mandatory disclosures page. This means they may not be deployed at all customer sites, but the data they generate is part of what TCC can store.\n- The vendor also provides medical billing services, so billing data appears to be a core part of the product, not an afterthought.\n- The website does not explicitly mention faxing, referral management, or patient messaging/secure messaging as distinct features (beyond the Bridge partnership and patient portal).\n- There is no mention of behavioral health-specific features, although therapists are listed as a target user group.\n- No mention of imaging storage (PACS) — only imaging orders. Introduction\nThis guide is written for third party developers, including patients, who are developing software applications for accessing Protected Health Information (PHI) based on this public API documentation which allows applications to query health data secured by a Data Holder. Data Holders wishing to publish such a public-facing API should have their Health IT vendor register as a developer integrator of EMR Direct Interoperability Engine services at\nhttps://www.emrdirect.com/subscribe-developer\n.\nALWAYS KEEP IN MIND THAT ONLINE DATA TRANSFER IS NOT A SUBSTITUTE FOR PERSON-TO-PERSON COMMUNICATION OF URGENT OR CRITICAL MEDICAL INFORMATION.\nThis documentation contains general information and important security information. General information will be marked as:\nNote:\nand important security information will be marked as:\nImportant:\n2. General Concepts\na. Application Access Requests\nThe API is a read-only RESTful HL7 FHIR® R4 API and follows the syntax described at\nhttps://hl7.org/fhir/R4/http.html\n.\nAll RESTful data access requests will be in the following format, in which the [base] URL will need to be obtained from the Data Holder, either directly or via directory information:\nGET [base]/[resource-specific parameters]…\nFHIR Bulk Data Access Group export is also supported as described in the\nBulk Data Access IG.\nb.\tConnecting to the server\nThe server is accessed by clients through an https connection.\nImportant:\nLocal customer security policies must be in place to prevent unauthorized monitoring or eavesdropping of connections to the server.\nImportant:\nOnly TLS 1.2 connections are accepted.  All plaintext connections will be refused.\nNote:\nPlease limit your connection frequency to a value appropriate for your use case. Connection attempts which are more frequent than permitted by the bandwidth allocation for the data resource are not allowed.\nc. Authentication – Obtaining an Access Token\nPrior to making API requests, the client application must obtain an Access Token from the associated Authorization Server. The client software must support either the OAuth 2.0 authorization code grant or client credentials grant flow as detailed in\nRFC 6749\n. For authorization code flow, a client ID and client secret are required. If the client application does not have a client ID and client secret for this purpose, the client application may obtain a client ID and client secret using the dynamic client registration protocol by submitting the required client information to the registration endpoint as detailed in\nRFC 7591\n, by accessing the registration endpoint URL with a browser and completing the online form, by completing the manual registration process at the EMR Direct website, or by using\nUDAP Dynamic Client Registration\n. Please see section 3(j) below for the list Client app attributes required for registration. For client credentials flow, a client ID and registered public key are required. Public keys for client credentials grant access may be registered using UDAP Dynamic Client Registration or by contacting the Data Holder directly.\nFor client applications using authorization code grants, end user authentication is performed using a username and authentication credentials provided by the Data Holder. A healthcare organization may reuse existing patient portal credentials for this purpose, in which case the authenticated username maps to a unique patient portal user on the resource holder’s side. The end user should obtain these credentials directly from the Data Holder healthcare organizations from which they wish to access data.\nNote:\nUsernames and authentication credentials are established and reset by the Data Holder.\nWhen an end user is directed to the authorization endpoint, the user will be presented with a login screen where they can enter their credentials for the healthcare organization they are accessing. If the correct credentials are supplied and the end user grants access to the client application, an authorization code will be returned to the client that the client application can use to obtain an access token through the token endpoint as described in RFC 6749. For client credentials flow, the application obtains an access token directly from the token endpoint without user interaction.\nEach healthcare organization has a unique base URL that is used to access its Authorization Server. The required endpoint URLs are of the following form:\nEndpoint\nURL\nAuthorization\nhttps://[baseOAuthURL]/authz\nToken\nhttps://[baseOAuthURL]/token\nRegistration\nhttps://[baseOAuthURL]/register\nManage\nhttps://[baseOAuthURL]/manage\nRevoke\nhttps://[baseOAuthURL]/revoke\nThe complete OAuth 2.0 endpoint URLs for a given FHIR endpoint are published in that Data Holder's well known SMART configuration endpoint, as per Section 8.2 of the HL7\nSMART App Launch specification\n.\nRefresh tokens: Client apps that use the authorization code flow and can securely store refresh tokens may request a refresh token by including the\noffline_access\nscope in the initial authorization endpoint request. Note that the end user may remove this scope during the authorization process. If a refresh token is permitted, it will be returned together with the access token as per\nRFC 6749\n, and can be exchanged for a new access token as per\nRFC 6749\n. Refresh token lifetime defaults to 90 days (but may be longer or shorter depending on the Data Holder's institutional policies). Each refresh token may be used once; a replacement refresh token will be issued when the client app uses a valid refresh token to obtain a new access token. Note that refresh tokens are not issued to Client apps that support client credentials code flow as these clients can authenticate directly to the token endpoint to obtain a new access token.\nPKCE support: Proof Key for Code Exchange (PKCE) as defined by\nRFC 7636\nis supported for Client apps that use the authorization code flow. The use of PKCE by such clients is optional. If used, Client apps must use only the\nS256\ncode challenge method. Please refer to\nRFC 7636\nfor details on how to use PKCE in requests to the authorization and token endpoints.\nAll requests to the API must include the access token transmitted in the Authorization header of the HTTP request as a bearer token as illustrated in\nRFC 6750\n. If the access token is missing, expired, or otherwise not valid for the requested operation, the API will return a 401 Unauthorized or 403 Forbidden response.\n3. API Details\na. Query a Specific Data Category Resource\nClient software must be capable of making HTTPS RESTful requests in accordance with the FHIR specification and consuming the FHIR Resources required by the United States Core Data for Interoperability (\nUSCDI\n) or the FHIR","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"db1f9056-e3a0-4b8f-aeb1-7872120eda75","uuid":"19561f28-faa5-40c6-be9b-295e514592aa","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"34678 /tmp/interop-api-docs.html\nOpen API Documentation | EMR Direct\nOpen API Documentation for Client Application Developers\nInteroperability Engine 2021\nUpdated Feb 5, 2026\n1. Introduction\nThis guide is written for third party developers, including patients, who are developing software applications for accessing Protected Health Information (PHI) based on this public API documentation which allows applications to query health data secured by a Data Holder. Data Holders wishing to publish such a public-facing API should have their Health IT vendor register as a developer integrator of EMR Direct Interoperability Engine services at\nhttps://www.emrdirect.com/subscribe-developer\n.\nALWAYS KEEP IN MIND THAT ONLINE DATA TRANSFER IS NOT A SUBSTITUTE FOR PERSON-TO-PERSON COMMUNICATION OF URGENT OR CRITICAL MEDICAL INFORMATION.\nThis documentation contains general information and important security information. General information will be marked as:\nNote:\nand important security information will be marked as:\nImportant:\n2. General Concepts\na. Application Access Requests\nThe API is a read-only RESTful HL7 FHIR® R4 API and follows the syntax described at\nhttps://hl7.org/fhir/R4/http.html\n.\nAll RESTful data access requests will be in the following format, in which the [base] URL will need to be obtained from the Data Holder, either directly or via directory information:\nGET [base]/[resource-specific parameters]…\nFHIR Bulk Data Access Group export is also supported as described in the\nBulk Data Access IG.\nb.\tConnecting to the server\nThe server is accessed by clients through an https connection.\nImportant:\nLocal customer security policies must be in place to prevent unauthorized monitoring or eavesdropping of connections to the server.\nImportant:\nOnly TLS 1.2 connections are accepted.  All plaintext connections will be refused.\nNote:\nPlease limit your connection frequency to a value appropriate for your use case. Connection attempts which are more frequent than permitted by the bandwidth allocation for the data resource are not allowed.\nc. Authentication – Obtaining an Access Token\nPrior to making API requests, the client application must obtain an Access Token from the associated Authorization Server. The client software must support either the OAuth 2.0 authorization code grant or client credentials grant flow as detailed in\nRFC 6749\n. For authorization code flow, a client ID and client secret are required. If the client application does not have a client ID and client secret for this purpose, the client application may obtain a client ID and client secret using the dynamic client registration protocol by submitting the required client information to the registration endpoint as detailed in\nRFC 7591\n, by accessing the registration endpoint URL with a browser and completing the online form, by completing the manual registration process at the EMR Direct website, or by using\nUDAP Dynamic Client Registration\n. Please see section 3(j) below for the list Client app attributes required for registration. For client credentials flow, a client ID and registered public key are required. Public keys for client credentials grant access may be registered using UDAP Dynamic Client Registration or by contacting the Data Holder directly.\nFor client applications using authorization code grants, end user authentication is performed using a username and authentication credentials provided by the Data Holder. A healthcare organization may reuse existing patient portal credentials for this purpose, in which case the authenticated username maps to a unique patient portal user on the resource holder’s side. The end user should obtain these credentials directly from the Data Holder healthcare organizations from which they wish to access data.\nNote:\nUsernames and authentication credentials are established and reset by the Data Holder.\nWhen an end user is directed to the authorization endpoint, the user will be presented with a login screen where they can enter their credentials for the healthcare organization they are accessing. If the correct credentials are supplied and the end user grants access to the client application, an authorization code will be returned to the client that the client application can use to obtain an access token through the token endpoint as described in RFC 6749. For client credentials flow, the application obtains an access token directly from the token endpoint without user interaction.\nEach healthcare organization has a unique base URL that is used to access its Authorization Server. The required endpoint URLs are of the following form:\nEndpoint\nURL\nAuthorization\nhttps://[baseOAuthURL]/authz\nToken\nhttps://[baseOAuthURL]/token\nRegistration\nhttps://[baseOAuthURL]/register\nManage\nhttps://[baseOAuthURL]/manage\nRevoke\nhttps://[baseOAuthURL]/revoke\nThe complete OAuth 2.0 endpoint URLs for a given FHIR endpoint are published in that Data Holder's well known SMART configuration endpoint, as per Section 8.2 of the HL7\nSMART App Launch specification\n.\nRefresh tokens: Client apps that use the authorization code flow and can securely store refresh tokens may request a refresh token by including the\noffline_access\nscope in the initial authorization endpoint request. Note that the end user may remove this scope during the authorization process. If a refresh token is permitted, it will be returned together with the access token as per\nRFC 6749\n, and can be exchanged for a new access token as per\nRFC 6749\n. Refresh token lifetime defaults to 90 days (but may be longer or shorter depending on the Data Holder's institutional policies). Each refresh token may be used once; a replacement refresh token will be issued when the client app uses a valid refresh token to obtain a new access token. Note that refresh tokens are not issued to Client apps that support client credentials code flow as these clients can authenticate directly to the token endpoint to obtain a new access token.\nPKCE support: Proof Key for Code Exchange (PKCE) as defined by\nRFC 7636\nis supported for Client apps that use the authorization code flow. The use of PKCE by such clients is optional. If used, Client apps must use only the\nS256\ncode challenge method. Please refer to\nRFC 7636\nfor details on how to use PKCE in requests to the authorization and token endpoints.\nAll requests to the API must include the access token transmitted in the Authorization header of the HTTP request as a bearer token as illustrated in\nRFC 6750\n. If the access token is missing, expired, or otherwise not valid for the requested operation, the API will return a 401 Unauthorized or 403 Forbidden response.\n3. API Details\na. Receive\n\nTOC/RS Validate\n\nTOC/RS Display\n\nEHI Export Single\n\nExpected Outcomes\nThe expected outcome for this measure is the Team Chart Concept product will create\ntransition of care/referral summary documents in the specified format and will be able to\nsuccessfully send them to the production system used by the testing partner. The\nexpected outcome for this measure is that 90% or more of the summary documents\ncreated and sent are done so successfully.\nThe expected outcome for this measure is the Team Chart Concept product will\nsuccessfully receive transition of care/referral summary documents sent from the\nproduction system used by the testing partner. The expected outcome for this measure is\nthat 90% or more of the summary documents sent are received successfully.\nThe expected outcome for this measure is the Team Chart Concept product will\nsuccessfully validate the transition of care/referral summary documents sent from the\nproduction system used by the testing partner. If the document is valid, the expected\noutcome is that the Team Chart Concept product will display the valid document to the\nuser. If the document is invalid, the expected outcome is that the Team Chart Concept\nproduct will notify the user the document is invalid and provide information regarding why\nthe document is invalid. The expected outcome for this measure is that 90% or more of\nthe summary documents validated will be displayed or provide information to the user\nregarding why the document is invalid.\nThe expected outcome for this measure is the Team Chart Concept product will\nsuccessfully display valid production transition of care/referral summary documents with\nthe ability for users to specify which sections of the document are displayed, to specify the\norder of sections to display, and to specify the number of sections to display. The expected\noutcome for this measure is that 90% or more of the summary documents will be displayed\nsuccessfully.\nThe expected outcome for this measure is the Team Chart Concept product will\nsuccessfully export medical records for a single patient to PDF and C-CDA file(s).\nThis is the expected result more than 90% of the time. Error rates will be tracked and\ntrended over time.\n5\n© 2024 Ulrich Medical Concepts, Inc.\n\n\f2929 Broadway St. | Paducah, KY 42001\n\nEHI Export Multiple\n\nCQM Record\n\nCQM Export\n\nAPI Patient\n\nAPI Data Category\n\nAPI Data All\n\nDirect Msg Measure\n\nThe expected outcome for this measure is the Team Chart Concept product will\nsuccessfully export medical records for a list of patients to PDF and C-CDA file(s).\nThis is the expected result more than 90% of the time. Error rates will be tracked and\ntrended over time.\nThe expected outcome for this measure is that the Team Chart Concept product can record\nthe data required each CQM it is certified for. Each CQM should display patients included\nin the Initial Population, Denominator, Numerator, Excluded List, and Exception List. Error\nrates for patients not correctly included in the Initial Population, Denominator, Numerator,\nExcluded List, or Exception List will be tracked and analyzed for correction.\nThe expected outcome for this measure is that the Team Chart Concept product can export\na QRDA - Category I XML file for each patient included in the Initial Population,\nDenominator, Numerator, Excluded List, and Exception List. Error rates for patients not\ncorrectly exported will be tracked and analyzed.\nThe expected outcome for this measure is that the Team Chart Concept product will\nprovide a patient ID to the requesting system if the requesting system is authorized and a\npatient is found using the demographic data provided. If the requesting system is not\nauthorized or no patient is found using the demographics specified, then no patient id will\nbe provided. The expected outcome for this measure is that the Team Chart Concept\nproduct will provide the identified patient id for all authorized requests made with\nmatching patient data.\nThe expected outcome for this measure is that the Team Chart Concept product will\nprovide patient data for the requested data category if the requesting system is\nauthorized, the patient is known to it, and the patient has data in the specified category. If\nthe requesting system is not authorized, the patient is not found using the id specified, or\nthe patient has no data in the data category specified, then no patient id will be provided.\nThe expected outcome for this measure is that the Team Chart Concept product will\nprovide the requested data for all authorized requests when the patient has data to return.\nThe expected outcome for this measure is that the Team Chart Concept product will\nprovide patient data for all data categories if the requesting system is authorized, the\npatient is known to it, and the patient has data in one or more categories. If the requesting\nsystem is not authorized, the patient is not found using the id specified, or the patient has\nno data in any of the categories, then no patient id will be provided. If the requesting\nsystem is authorized and the patient is found using the id specified but the patient does\nnot have data in some categories, then a response will be provided with information in the\ncategories that the patient has data for. The expected outcome for this measure is that the\nTeam Chart Concept product will provide the requested data for all authorized requests\nwhen the patient has data to return.\nThe expected outcome for this measure is that the Team Chart Concept product can\nsuccessfully send one or more direct messages from one production client environment to\nanother production client environment and that it can receive one or more Direct\nMessages using the same two production environments. The number of Direct Messages\nsent and received will be tracked. Any errors that occur will be tracked and analyzed.\n\n6\n© 2024 Ulrich Medical Concepts, Inc.\n\n\f2929 Broadway St. | Paducah, KY 42001\n\nSCHEDULE OF KEY MILESTONES\nList of key milestones, their associated care settings, and dates/timeframes.\nKey Milestone\nFinalization of RWT plan.\nSubmission of final RWT plan to ONC-ACB.\nIdentify clients and partners to participate in RWT.\nConduct tests as identified by measures and record outcomes.\nComparison of actual outcomes to expected outcomes.\nAnalysis of testing results.\nCreation of RWT report.\nSubmission of RWT report to ONC-ACB.\n\nCare Setting\nAmbulatory\nAmbulatory\nAmbulatory\nAmbulatory\nAmbulatory\nAmbulatory\nAmbulatory\nAmbulatory\n\nDate/Timeframe\n09/01/24– 10/15/24\n10/15/24 - 12/15/24\n09/01/24 – 12/31/25\n01/01/25 – 10/31/25\n01/01/25 – 10/31/25\n11/01/25 – 01/15/26\n11/01/25 – 01/15/26\n01/15/2026\n\nATTESTATION\nThis Real World Testing plan is complete with all required elements, including measures that address all\ncertification criteria and care settings. WordPress site behind Sucuri/Cloudproxy CDN. Page loaded directly, no redirects.\n\n### Step 2: Fetch and examine the page\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://ulrichmedicalconcepts.com/home/the-ehr/meaningful-use/cost-disclosure-and-transparency/\" \\\n  -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o cost-disclosure-and-transparency.html\n```\nThe page is titled \"Disclosure and Transparency\" — a mandatory ONC compliance/cost disclosure page built with Elementor on WordPress. It contains:\n- Certified EHR vendor/product information table\n- Clinical Quality Measures list\n- Certification Criteria table (all 26+ criteria listed)\n- A single footnote about the (b)(10) EHI export\n- Cost transparency information\n- Application Access API additional information with links to Interoperability Engine docs\n\n### Step 3: Identify the EHI export documentation\nThe entire (b)(10) EHI export documentation on this page consists of a single footnote attached to the certification criteria listing:\n\n> **§170.315(b)(10) Electronic Health Information (EHI) Export\\***\n>\n> *EHI can be exported in PDF or C-CDA format. Records for a patient are exported to one or more PDF/C-CDA files. The files created by the export are saved to a folder specified by the user.*\n\nThat is the complete EHI export documentation. There are no links to a data dictionary, schema, user guide, sample files, or any additional technical documentation about the export.\n\n### Step 4: Check linked resources\nThe page links to three external resources under \"Application Access API Additional Information\":\n1. **Interoperability Engine Open API Documentation**: https://www.interopengine.com/2021/open-api-documentation.html — This is the FHIR R4 API documentation from EMR Direct. It covers the (g)(10) standardized API, not the (b)(10) EHI export. The documentation describes a read-only RESTful FHIR R4 API with OAuth 2.0 authentication, supporting US Core Implementation Guide resources. No mention of EHI, (b)(10), or bulk data export for all health information.\n2. **Interoperability Engine Open API Terms of Use**: https://www.interopengine.com/open-api-terms.html — API terms of service, not relevant to EHI export.\n3. **FHIR R4 Endpoints JSON**: https://appstudio.interopengine.com/partner/fhirR4endpoints-umc.json — Returns a nearly empty FHIR Bundle: `{\"resourceType\":\"Bundle\",\"type\":\"collection\",\"timestamp\":\"2026-02-07T14:37:24-08:00\"}` with no actual endpoint entries. This suggests no UMC customers have active FHIR API integrations.\n\n### Step 5: Explore the broader UMC site\nChecked the sitemap (`/wp-sitemap-posts-page-1.xml`) for all pages on the site. Relevant pages checked:\n\n- **/home/the-ehr/interoperability/** — General interoperability marketing content about HIE connectivity, cancer registry reporting, and HIMSS conference participation. No EHI export details.\n- **/home/the-ehr/meaningful-use/** — Marketing page about Meaningful Use incentives and cancer registry reporting. No EHI export details.\n- **/onc-real-world-testing/** — Links to Real World Testing plan and results PDFs (2022-2025).\n\n### Step 6: Examine Real World Testing documents for EHI export details\nThe 2025 RWT Plan PDF (https://elearning.ulrichmedicalconcepts.com/RWT/2025_RWT_Plan_Ulrich_Medical_Concepts.pdf) contains the most detail about the EHI export:\n\n**EHI Export Single** (§170.315(b)(10)):\n> \"This test is completed by selecting a single patient in the Team Chart Concept product as user that has permission to complete a chart export and running the chart export routine to export the patient's chart to PDF and C-CDA file(s). If a PDF is generated the result has been met successfully.\"\n\n**EHI Export Multiple** (§170.315(b)(10)):\n> \"This test is completed by retrieving a list of patients in the Team Chart Concept product as user that has permission to complete a chart export and running the chart export routine to export each patient's chart to PDF and C-CDA file(s). If multiple PDFs are generated the result has been met successfully.\"\n\n**Notable finding**: The 2025 RWT Results Report (4 pages) covers outcomes for API Patient, API Data All, and API Data Category — but **omits the EHI Export Single and EHI Export Multiple outcomes entirely**. The results report only has 3 measures in its outcomes table despite the plan including 12 measures. All three tested measures note: \"No customers opted to implement.\"\n\n### Step 7: Full-page screenshot\nCaptured a full-page screenshot of the disclosure page using Chrome DevTools.\n\n## What Was Found\n\nThe EHI export documentation for Team Chart Concept is extraordinarily minimal. The entire public-facing description of the (b)(10) export consists of a **single sentence footnote** on the vendor's mandatory disclosure page:\n\n> *EHI can be exported in PDF or C-CDA format. Records for a patient are exported to one or more PDF/C-CDA files. The files created by the export are saved to a folder specified by the user.*\n\nFrom the RWT plan, we learn slightly more: the export is called a \"chart export routine\" that can be run for a single patient or a list of patients, and it requires a user with \"permission to complete a chart export.\" The success criterion is simply \"if a PDF is generated.\"\n\nThere is no:\n- Data dictionary or field listing\n- Schema definition\n- Export format specification beyond \"PDF or C-CDA\"\n- Sample export files\n- User guide or instructions\n- API specification for the export\n- Description of what data is included in the export\n- Description of what data is excluded from the export\n- Information about C-CDA template conformance or profile\n- Documentation of the export's relationship to the FHIR API\n\nThe linked Interoperability Engine documentation covers the FHIR R4 API for (g)(10) compliance and is a completely separate mechanism from the (b)(10) export. The FHIR endpoints JSON for UMC customers is empty, suggesting no active FHIR integrations.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nBased on the product research, Team Chart Concept manages:\n- **Clinical data**: Demographics, encounters, problem lists, medications, allergies, immunizations, vitals, health maintenance, implantable devices, clinical decision support, quality measures\n- **Order data**: Lab orders/results, medication orders, diagnostic imaging orders\n- **Prescribing data**: E-prescriptions (via NewCrop), medication history\n- **Practice management**: Scheduling, billing, claims, financial reports, ICD-10 codes\n- **Document management**: Scanned documents, office forms, patient chart documents\n- **Patient engagement**: Portal interactions, patient communications\n- **Interoperability data**: C-CDA documents, HL7 messages, FHIR resources\n\n**Coverage assessment is essentially impossible** because the vendor provides no data dictionary, field listing, or any description of what the export includes. The documentation says \"records for a patient are exported\" but never defines what constitutes a \"record.\"\n\n**What we can infer:**\n- **PDF export**: A PDF rendering of the patient chart likely captures whatever is displayed in the EHR's chart view — probably clinical notes, demographics, medications, problem lists, allergies, vitals, and lab results. However, PDFs are not computable. Billing data, scheduling data, and structured coded data are likely lost or flattened.\n- **C-CDA export**: C-CDA documents follow a defined standard (CCD, CCD-A, or referral note templates). If this is the same C-CDA used for Transitions of Care (b)(1), it would cover the USCDI subset: demographics, problems, medications, allergies, immunizations, vital signs, procedures, results, and care team. This would miss billing data, scheduling, custom forms, document attachments, and any specialty-specific data.\n\n**Likely missing domains** (based on typical PDF/C-CDA limitations):\n- Billing records and claims data (TCC is an integrated PM system — billing is a core function)\n- Scheduling/appointment data\n- Custom \"User Defined Records\" (a TCC feature for ad-hoc reporting)\n- Scanned document attachments and digitized historical records\n- Lab order details (as distinct from results)\n- E-prescribing history from NewCrop integration\n- Patient portal interaction data\n- Direct messaging correspondence\n- Cancer registry submission data\n- Health maintenance alerts and reminders\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses two formats:\n1. **PDF** — A non-computable, human-readable rendering. Useful for visual review but cannot be programmatically parsed, queried, or imported into another system with any reliability. The PDF format fundamentally cannot satisfy the spirit of (b)(10), which requires export of data in a form that preserves its structure and utility.\n2. **C-CDA** — A structured clinical document standard (HL7 CDA R2). While computable, C-CDA is designed for clinical summaries, not comprehensive data export. It covers a subset of clinical data aligned with USCDI/US Core but lacks representations for billing, scheduling, custom forms, and many specialty-specific data types.\n\nNeither format is appropriate for a comprehensive EHI export of all electronic health information stored by an integrated EHR and practice management system. A database dump, CSV export of all tables, or even a FHIR Bulk Data export with custom extensions would be more appropriate.\n\nThe RWT plan describes the export success criterion as \"if a PDF is generated\" — the bar for success is whether a file was created, not whether it contains all EHI.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\nThe documentation quality is **extremely poor** — effectively nonexistent:\n- No data dictionary\n- No field-level definitions\n- No data types, value sets, or constraints\n- No worked examples or sample export files\n- No user guide for performing the export\n- No description of what data is included or excluded\n- A developer could not implement an import based on this documentation\n- The documentation consists of a single footnote\n\nThe RWT plan adds marginal detail (describing the export as a \"chart export routine\" with user permission requirements and single/multiple patient modes), but this is operational testing documentation, not export format documentation.\n\nThis is clearly a compliance checkbox exercise, not a genuine effort to document the EHI export.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\nThere is no structured documentation to assess. The vendor has:\n- **Zero** tables, fields, or columns documented\n- **Zero** relationships or entity descriptions\n- **Zero** value set or coded field documentation\n- **Zero** versioning or change history\n- **Zero** schema or format specification\n\nThe vendor's certified criteria include (b)(10) since December 2019. Over six years later, the public documentation still consists of a single sentence.\n\n### The (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Situation\n\nIn this case, the vendor does **not** conflate (b)(10) with (g)(10) — the EHI export (PDF/C-CDA files saved locally) is clearly distinct from the FHIR API (Interoperability Engine). However, the FHIR API (g)(10) is equally undocumented from UMC's perspective — they rely entirely on EMR Direct's generic Interoperability Engine documentation, and the FHIR endpoints JSON shows zero active customer integrations.\n\nThe real issue is that the vendor appears to have done the absolute minimum: a basic \"chart export to PDF\" function and a C-CDA export (which they likely already had for Transitions of Care (b)(1) compliance), with no thought given to whether these formats actually capture all electronic health information in the designated record set.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://ulrichmedicalconcepts.com/home/the-ehr/meaningful-use/cost-disclosure-and-transparency/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (static HTML, works with curl)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (everything is on the single registered page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none (Sucuri/Cloudproxy CDN, but no blocking)\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- The FHIR endpoints JSON (fhirR4endpoints-umc.json) is effectively empty — a Bundle with no entries\n- The 2025 RWT Results Report omits EHI Export outcomes despite the plan including them\n- No additional EHI export documentation found anywhere on the UMC site (checked sitemap, interoperability page, meaningful use page, and real-world testing page)\n- No downloadable PDFs, ZIPs, or data dictionary files linked from the disclosure page\n- The Interoperability Engine API docs are generic EMR Direct documentation, not UMC-specific\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":589,"cache_read_input_tokens":77727,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":589},"output_tokens":25,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"db1f9056-e3a0-4b8f-aeb1-7872120eda75","uuid":"9d7b4bd1-8551-43fb-99c6-717fb9e60fff"}

The PDF format fundamentally cannot satisfy the spirit of (b)(10), which requires export of data in a form that preserves its structure and utility.\n2. **C-CDA** — A structured clinical document standard (HL7 CDA R2). While computable, C-CDA is designed for clinical summaries, not comprehensive data export. It covers a subset of clinical data aligned with USCDI/US Core but lacks representations for billing, scheduling, custom forms, and many specialty-specific data types.\n\nNeither format is appropriate for a comprehensive EHI export of all electronic health information stored by an integrated EHR and practice management system. A database dump, CSV export of all tables, or even a FHIR Bulk Data export with custom extensions would be more appropriate.\n\nThe RWT plan describes the export success criterion as \"if a PDF is generated\" — the bar for success is whether a file was created, not whether it contains all EHI.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\nThe documentation quality is **extremely poor** — effectively nonexistent:\n- No data dictionary\n- No field-level definitions\n- No data types, value sets, or constraints\n- No worked examples or sample export files\n- No user guide for performing the export\n- No description of what data is included or excluded\n- A developer could not implement an import based on this documentation\n- The documentation consists of a single footnote\n\nThe RWT plan adds marginal detail (describing the export as a \"chart export routine\" with user permission requirements and single/multiple patient modes), but this is operational testing documentation, not export format documentation.\n\nThis is clearly a compliance checkbox exercise, not a genuine effort to document the EHI export.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\nThere is no structured documentation to assess. The vendor has:\n- **Zero** tables, fields, or columns documented\n- **Zero** relationships or entity descriptions\n- **Zero** value set or coded field documentation\n- **Zero** versioning or change history\n- **Zero** schema or format specification\n\nThe vendor's certified criteria include (b)(10) since December 2019. Over six years later, the public documentation still consists of a single sentence.\n\n### The (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Situation\n\nIn this case, the vendor does **not** conflate (b)(10) with (g)(10) — the EHI export (PDF/C-CDA files saved locally) is clearly distinct from the FHIR API (Interoperability Engine). However, the FHIR API (g)(10) is equally undocumented from UMC's perspective — they rely entirely on EMR Direct's generic Interoperability Engine documentation, and the FHIR endpoints JSON shows zero active customer integrations.\n\nThe real issue is that the vendor appears to have done the absolute minimum: a basic \"chart export to PDF\" function and a C-CDA export (which they likely already had for Transitions of Care (b)(1) compliance), with no thought given to whether these formats actually capture all electronic health information in the designated record set.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://ulrichmedicalconcepts.com/home/the-ehr/meaningful-use/cost-disclosure-and-transparency/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (static HTML, works with curl)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (everything is on the single registered page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none (Sucuri/Cloudproxy CDN, but no blocking)\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- The FHIR endpoints JSON (fhirR4endpoints-umc.json) is effectively empty — a Bundle with no entries\n- The 2025 RWT Results Report omits EHI Export outcomes despite the plan including them\n- No additional EHI export documentation found anywhere on the UMC site (checked sitemap, interoperability page, meaningful use page, and real-world testing page)\n- No downloadable PDFs, ZIPs, or data dictionary files linked from the disclosure page\n- The Interoperability Engine API docs are generic EMR Direct documentation, not UMC-specific\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
